The direct MOS injector also needs a nonlinear weight mapping across PVT. Solver-controlled fractions0.4/0.6 produce a1.62× spread in their charge ratio across selected corners; scalar gain adjustment cannot align both. A physical per-macro calibration/reference or a more stable charge source must be demonstrated before the ideal float weights can be bound to a gate DAC or replicated layout.

A fixed pulse width per PLIF stage may extend the current-amplitude encoding without making each static weight's pulse long. On the three sampled float patches, an illustrative worst-corner width ceiling is444.1ns and optimistic p99 serialized pulse service is≤15.74µs per stage. The physical positive event is close to linear with width through450ns at zero state, but has5.35% transfer variation over prepared±0.9V differential. This remains a range/service candidate only; no full weighted fan-in, negative-sign table, physical calibration, state-noise budget or graph timing closure exists.

A signed ten-event physical sequence now bounds repeated transfer at one gate setting. At40ns same-sign accumulation is0.0731% low, but alternating signs leave2.135µV differential residual; at450ns the corresponding errors are0.822% and0.2657mV. These residuals are measured against a simulator no-event control, and the positive/negative branches are nominally matched. Actual float-weighted fan-in and PLIF decision/reset behavior remain the next architecture gate.

The first-touch candidate now has a bounded actual float-weighted fan-in screen at SS/0°C. A stage-fixed40.253ns pulse and ideal gate levels represent each signed epoch58 weight; a sparse single-event charge table supplies the nonlinear map. Physical MIM/TG/four-MOS accumulation was simulated for370,322 and329 event sites. Log interpolation of the directly measured stage-width curve improves the370-event sum to within0.39–1.66% but leaves0.662mV event-order spread. The329-event site at32.623µV target has9–22nV deterministic error, while a nearly cancelling322-event site changes nanovolt sign with ordering. This topology remains a **schematic event-source candidate**: the gate table has no physical DAC/reference or trim loop, the capacitor pair is area/noise prohibitive as a replicated store, and no credible switched readout or PLIF update loop is attached. The next integration boundary is a physically generated calibrated gate plus noise-aware state sensing and beta/reset/adaptation, followed by 5ms service sizing.

Selected TT/27°C and FF/85°C device runs preserve the370-event sum within0.03–1.67% only when each receives its own externally measured ideal gate table. Reusing the SS table at TT drives the contribution to−141/−148mV instead of−52.45mV; at FF the ordered output ranges from+25.6 to−596mV. The next analog module must generate and refresh a local or shared calibration reference for the nonlinear gate-to-charge relation under operating temperature and process variation. This requirement adds area, service and noise costs that are absent from the current ideal-driver schematic; no transistor implementation of that module has passed.

An analog weight storage screen now tests two physical connections to that injector. A sampled gate capacitor connected only during an event suffers gate-node charge sharing; enlarging its two MIM capacitors to100µm each still gives−2.90% transfer error after5ms and≈2,874.56mm² bare area for folded PLIF weights. Keeping the MOS gates attached and steering source/drain current avoids that connection, but rail-clamped idle source nodes cause a21.93mV neutral switching packet. VCM idle clamping cuts the neutral packet to0.351µV at one gate fraction, while continuous current and stored-gate drift remain:10/20/50/100µm pairs change the gate-dependent event+91/+20/+3.1/+0.77% over5ms. The next circuit boundary is a physically isolated/buffered and refreshed analog weight reference with measured service, power and noise. Neither sampled-gate mux nor the current drain-steered variant is a chosen weight cell.

An existing transistor OTA provides a preliminary shared-buffer boundary. In SS/0°C DC unity feedback with ideal biases, its selected p-gate at0.936V is within24µV, while low n-gate levels0.48/0.30V miss by1.25/2.67mV. Driving the physical injector gate loads after an off-rail step, the p output settles in about0.1µs but the n output takes roughly7–9µs and overshoots. This means a shared buffer needs early weight prefetch and more parallel service than a50ns direct event train assumes. The OTA is not yet connected to a sampled analog weight store or a physical calibration loop, so it remains outside the selected physical datapath.


## Analog module implementation update — 2026-09-23

Historical ownership note: superseded by the full implementation delegation above. Initial module library in asic/analog/modules.spice now covers sampled PLIF, membrane hold, signed synapse, leak target, threshold/subtractive reset, square/root energy, triangular adaptation, peer inhibition, class integration and argmax, plus CMOS primitives. The design YAML binds roles and learned exported constants to all 25 actors. It is a mixed-level prototype: only switches/inverters/comparator use actual SKY130 transistor models; behavioral components are not physically implemented or power-complete.

Five bounded ngspice benches pass. The first integrated PLIF attempt exposed destructive charge sharing between state and snapshot capacitors. A labelled ideal isolation buffer fixes functional charge transfer; its transistor implementation remains required. Eight-bin maximum state error is 0.004235 model units with a provisional 0.02 smoke tolerance, not an accuracy target. Two capacitors plus a buffer are currently required by this prototype; do not count it as one bare capacitor per neuron.

Comparator stimulus mean VDD power is 1.48973 uW over 5 us, at SKY130 TT and 1.8 V. This excludes bias/reference generation and layout parasitics; do not scale it as whole-model or whole-PLIF power. All other behavioral modules retain unknown physical power/area/latency. Result evidence: artifacts/analog_asic/results/test_results.json and per-bench logs/traces. No physical analog layout, DRC/LVS, extracted PPA, 5 ms retention test or whole-model accuracy validation exists.

Models: https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_pr at f62031a1be9aefe902d6d54cddd6f59b57627436; preserved upstream files/licenses. SKY130 is a feasibility choice, distinct from digital ASAP7; cross-technology numbers are not direct architecture comparisons.

A bounded repeated-event test of the 12-bit TT-trimmed shared R-2R found stable per-event transfer at two gate settings: ten-event TT/27°C errors are−2.13% and−1.41%, and negative polarity mirrors positive. Five events with TT codes at SS/0°C and FF/27°C stay−3.44 to−1.00%. The ten-event SS run collapses at a later pulse; the five-event SS run completes. This indicates event count alone is not worsening the two sampled transfers, but does not qualify the rail DAC: ideal resistors, no noise/mismatch/extraction, only two bias points, and no float-weighted near-canceling fan-in or readout feedback. The next circuit boundary is signed full-float weight fan-in through physical rails and a calibrated nonlinear transfer.

The measured geometry/bias library now spans13 MOS width scales and five shared analog rails (65 measured positive-event charge points). A local full-float nearest-level replay reaches1.543% final_fuse,0.532% enc1,2.263% context2 mismatch. The charge points are nonmonotonic with width at every bias, so treat them as a finite measured table; the continuous-width interpretation remains unverified. This replay uses a width/corner-independent TT/27°C R-2R gain correction, one positive event from zero state, frozen presynaptic traces and no actor feedback. The next integration gate is actual signed full-float fan-in with order/cancellation and physical per-event charge, before threshold/readout feedback or layout.

At the actual 40.253ns final-fusion pulse width and2ns gate transition, a measured eight-bias/13-geometry lookup uses full float32 weights directly, without QAT. The bounded local replay has0.43% final-fusion,0.29% enc1 and1.41% context2 spike mismatch, with no high-side weight clipping. A physical shared-geometry-bank 370-event test nevertheless delivers8.8–10.0% less charge than target and shows0.660mV event-order spread. The table's isolated event charges predict the sum within0.24%, so investigate state-dependent sequence transfer and inactive-bank loading. The physical fan-in deck uses PWL gate sources adjusted from TT/27°C R-2R ratios; it is not the R-2R circuit itself. This remains an unresolved analog integration gate ahead of readout, all-state storage, service and layout.

A per-event series-TG isolation probe is not viable as written: the isolated one-event deck has a sign reversal and a +20.871mV response to a −0.924mV target; its 50-event response is −137.364mV versus +1.261mV ideal. Treat the mechanism as unresolved floating-node charge sharing/feedthrough or polarity interaction. Any follow-up must first qualify isolated single-event sign and scale before extending the event count.

The current best completed maximum-fanin schematic result uses the shared 13-geometry MOS bank and42.266ns event pulses in a50.253ns slot. At SS/0°C, the actual370-event float sequence ends4.0–5.4% below ideal with0.718mV order spread. The lookup and shared-R-2R correction remain calibrated for40.253ns, so the width increase is provisional. A per-synapse isolation probe matches a single event only after precharge/switch sizing but fails on signed accumulation. Re-measure the event library at42.266ns and repeat mapping/order tests; do not promote either result to a qualified float synapse yet.

## Active synapse method — matched analog charge mapping (2026-09-30)

The current leading Sky130 synapse is a shared 13-geometry MOS injector pair connected to a differential MIM state. Presynaptic activations expand to signed charge events from the ordinary float32 model; the learned weight controls analog gate bias over the measured transfer map, with pulse duration used only to extend below the smallest full-width charge level. Full fan-in is bounded at 370 events / 50.253ns slots in the current schematic stress bench. This timing is a compact charge-accumulation experiment, not a proposed real-time presynaptic schedule.

The transfer map is calibrated with the same shared bank loaded, at the deployed 42.2657ns event width, and reproduces the 370-event mapped float sum within−0.0044%. PDK transient results at SS/0°C are within−1.65/−0.68/−1.21% over three event orders. No resistor ladder or calibrated reference is instantiated in that fan-in deck; its correction is applied as an ideal gate command. Threshold, reset, beta/hold, state feedback, noise/mismatch, physical event driver, extraction and area remain open. The synapse is a promising schematic block, not an accepted PLIF or scalable macro. No QAT or weight quantization is used.

## Charge-bucket comparison update — 2026-09-30

The continuous single-ended OTA charge bucket can transfer a100-packet signed train over5ms, but it needs a separate neutral reference to cancel its drift and switching baseline. That reference is highly sensitive to tiny bias differences, and physical startup control still leaves a multi-millivolt deck-dependent branch state. It is not a credible replacement for the current full-float direct-MOS event source or a complete PLIF store/readout. Continue with a shared differential front end containing physical common-mode control and correlated offset cancellation, and a separate dense analog state-storage feasibility study before scale-up. See the new result section and the machine-readable architecture boundary.

The 329-event float-weighted fan-in has now been connected to the physical clocked latch on a10µm differential MIM state pair. Nominal decisions on both sides of the trained threshold work under ideal threshold initialization, but a near-zero diagnostic threshold shift exposes a0.222µV float/physical decision boundary discrepancy. The independent-pair reset-noise proxy is192µV RMS at0°C, while the trained site's margin is4.087µV. This cell is a connected deterministic reference for a future readout, not a qualified neuron or selected all-state store. The comparator, threshold source, beta/reset, and state representation must be designed together with physical noise and5ms service constraints before a macro can be selected.

The float trace and two full-frame range/noise replays show why a direct linear voltage enlargement is insufficient. With each actor's observed maximum fitted to±0.9V, final-fusion small threshold margins coexist with candidate states up to13,801 model units. Increasing linear gain32–64× while hard-clipping accumulated state improves the independent10µm-pair reset-noise proxy but still changes about20–22% of pixel classes and4.6–4.9% of final-fusion spikes on the two screened records. At100× the clipped network collapses. A future analog cell must encode fine near-threshold differences and large accumulated state in the same5ms update semantics, with a physically realizable nonlinear/split-range store or another verified representation. This requirement supplements the shared differential common-mode/offset-control gate; it does not select a compander circuit or change the original float model.

An ideal `asinh` state-voltage map shows a mathematical upper bound for nonlinear range allocation: on the same two complete frames and independent10µm-pair noise proxy,~1010× near-zero voltage gain reduces final-fusion spike mismatch to2.31/2.41% and pixel disagreement to12–13%, while zero-noise float semantics remain exact. It is still short of faithful spike equivalence. Its state-dependent event increment, inverse, wide-range storage, beta/reset and threshold handling have no physical SKY130 implementation here. The additive direct-MOS injector cannot be carried into this encoding unchanged. A concrete analog split-range/charge-domain cell must be drawn and measured before nonlinear encoding is an architectural choice.

The next concrete analog research cell is a charge-preserving range transition for an eight-segment piecewise membrane map. Each segment has constant state-to-voltage slope, but record0 final-fusion slopes vary by~4,496× across the full span. Software replay is close to the smooth bound yet remains only ideal; the physical cell must carry a signed weighted event across one segment boundary without injecting spurious charge, then hold the resulting differential through a5ms bin. The current direct-MOS bank can serve as an event-source reference but does not automatically provide the needed state-dependent voltage increment. A shared differential offset-controlled readout and dense state storage are still separate open gates.

The actual float-weight event-demand screen shows why a state-aware source matters: the near-threshold and cancellation sites need at most0.858/1.220mV per ideal encoded event, but the maximum-fan-in site needs up to342mV for one reverse-order event and has16 of370 events above the measured16.624mV direct-MOS one-event calibration. A fixed-charge source with a huge per-state capacitor ladder is not an area-feasible way to cover the4,496× slope span. Investigate a modest analog state store with controlled signed event charge and physically enforce boundary continuity; extra event slots are only an arithmetic possibility until the state-dependent transfer and service schedule are simulated.

The bounded physical first-segment actual-weight screen supports the small-signal source only conditionally. The near-threshold329-event site accumulates23.699mV to within42.4µV across three orders, but the322-event high-cancellation site cannot reliably preserve a+5.44µV signed sum: at55ns its completed order errors span+11.6 to−67.1µV. A manually inferred one-geometry state/polarity gate command reduces order spread without closing the shuffled or reverse errors. Hence the next source design needs calibration as a function of weight, geometry, sign and stored voltage, not merely a scalar gain or a state-sign trim. The cell still lacks a physical state sensor, range transitions, beta/threshold/reset and low-noise dense state storage.

The actual high-cancellation mix includes125 geometry-4 events out of322 and has median effective gate fraction0.677. Its physical shuffled prefix error reaches−20.6µV by event120 and persists through the final cancellation. This error is much larger than its+5.44µV ideal net contribution, while the earlier independent10µm-pair thermal reset proxy is larger still. The architecture cannot be promoted by source calibration alone; a viable analog state representation must jointly address signed transfer, noise,5ms hold and storage across30.976m identities.

The first SKY130 physical range-transition bench shows how much supporting circuitry this needs. A10µm fine MIM plus13.3µm extension per side can carry a manually timed positive100nA crossing close to its two-capacitance ideal only when the extension is *ideally precharged to the boundary voltage*. Leaving it at VCM would cut a112.5mV differential to41.0mV. The ideally prepared event signal then loses6.82% relative to its no-event branch over5ms. This is one segment boundary, one positive current pulse, and no physical range detector, precharge buffer, weighted MOS source, reverse crossing or noise. Keep the architecture unselected until the precharge and leakage problems are solved with actual circuits and service/area costs.

The boundary voltage is fixed for each segment, so the first physical precharge candidate should distribute/program that reference rather than track each state's arbitrary voltage. In the bounded ideal-source stress,±1mV differential reference error causes about±0.635mV final state error at the positive crossing. A real reference must also charge the extension without corrupting the fine state, reach the target before the event crosses, and remain accurate across process and local mismatch. This does not remove the range detector, reverse crossing,5ms leakage or noise gates.

The first range-switch mechanism is **not scalable as a per-state capacitor bank**. To get the ideal eight-range slope span by capacitance alone, a10µm fine MIM's207.6fF must grow to~933pF per side in the final range. The SKY130 typical MIM formula gives~683µm square per side, or~28.9m² bare area for two sides at every30.976m logical states. The conditional one-boundary result should guide switch/reference characterization only. Retain nonlinear range allocation as a software target, and redirect physical research to variable signed event charge on a modest state capacitor or shared active charge integration; both must demonstrate actual weighted-event addition, low-noise state/readout, beta/reset and5ms service before a macro is chosen.

A nominal TT transistor-gate storage probe finds0.650–0.797pF from100µm² drawn gate at0.6–1.2V, versus0.207pF from equal-area MIM, using matched current-pulse subtraction. Its 5ms off-center sampled-state drift is3.7–4.6mV and its calculated independent-pair thermal scale is102–113µV. The full-inventory bare-gate area at the earlier10µm MIM-pair capacitance is optimistically1,613–1,975mm²; legal diffusion/switch/routing area and analog refresh are not included. MOS gate storage therefore remains a compact-capacitance research cell, not a solution to the fine-state/noise/service gates. Source: `sky130_mos_state_cap_boundary.json`.

Conditional one-pole service arithmetic now bounds naive off-center full-inventory refresh: at an illustrative3µV drift target, each of30.976m states would need a visit every3.26–4.00µs, yielding7.74–9.51million channels at1µs/state before read/write costs. Voltage-independent endpoint decay correction also fails at intermediate prepared states by22–258µV over5ms. These are TT diagnostic bounds, not a measured refresh system or accepted model-error budget. A useful next shared analog bucket must either keep the small state value from being lost/noisy or use a realizable state-dependent representation and compensate leakage during the normal update, then close signed-event/threshold/beta/reset and service gates before layout.

Two series TGs with a floating or MIM-held middle node do not improve5ms retention at±0.3V: state drift remains3.7–4.6mV. The bare ideal-initialized MOS gate does hold in the nominal model; isolated off NFET and PFET branches pull it toward opposite rails by several millivolts. This points to leakage of the transistor physically attached to the state node, which a second series switch cannot remove. A new state-cell topology must change that attached-device path or tolerate/compensate it with physically qualified precision. The tested stack is not selected for layout.

Tying an off switch's body to the stored node is not qualified: the largest simulated drifts occur outside or at the documented body-bias range. The in-range single-device body cases still drift1.9–2.4mV over5ms, with no physical initialization or isolated-well layout. The special SRAM pass devices described in the official SKY130 documentation cannot be used outside their hard-IP arrays. A new switch/process proposal must supply a supported analog device, legal well/body layout, and measured corner/noise/retention behavior before macro layout. This keeps the shared active charge bucket as a research direction, not a completed state-store architecture.

A public-model5V-gate TG substitution on the same100µm² MOS state shows+4.553/−3.715mV5ms drift from0.6/1.2V, versus+4.587/−3.714mV for the1.8V TG. Longer programming reaches each starting voltage within0.76mV, but the hold gate remains open. The model snapshots may differ in revision, and no legal high-voltage layout or extracted view exists. Changing nominal device voltage alone is not a qualified storage fix.

The measured TT hold curve now spans most of the candidate0–1.8V state rail and gives a grounded software sensitivity comparison. On two full-float frames, ideal piecewise R10000 encoding plus interpolated physical MOS drift and an illustrative113µV independent reset-noise proxy gives~2.9% final-fusion spike mismatch and~85% pixel agreement, with mean IoU close to the baseline on these two frames. The same small capacitor under a linear map gives~13.7% spike mismatch and mean IoU~0.119 against~0.20 float. The piecewise cell is not physically selected: it still requires state-dependent `f(m+Δm)−f(m)` signed charge, nonlinear decoding, physical beta/subtractive reset/threshold, calibrated gain and a low-noise readout. A full-range per-state capacitance bank has already failed area. Next screen an active state-dependent event-charge law on a modest store against actual signed weights and range crossings, while retaining population service/noise/area gates ahead of layout.
The matched-load re-run corrects the preliminary injector replay: its first transfer map did not include the full off-device load. Using a104-point map measured with all13 geometries connected, the322-event high-cancellation site has maximum sampled prefix error0.159mV forward/reverse and27.9µV shuffled, with final errors+21.6/−15.3/+5.13µV against+5.44µV target. A full-bank ±31mV state sweep changes geometry4 transfer by less than about1% at gate fractions0.8–0.9. Reverse ordering still changes the final sign, so the update path is not order-robust. Investigate geometry/bias interpolation and implement physical bias and state sensing before layout.
A first ideal state-dependent bias compensation sweep uses the high-cancellation trace to set a0.0022/V slope. Its322-event forward/reverse/shuffled final errors fall to3.79/3.32/3.00µV, with no sampled sign errors; maximum trajectory error is still0.176mV. Applied unchanged to a separate329-event near-threshold-scale trace, it does not improve final error (0.156–0.164mV) over the no-correction forward/reverse runs (0.160/0.124mV). These are ideal state-aware controls and only two selected sites. The compensation is not selected as a global circuit law; map its dependence on geometry, bias and state range, then evaluate a transistor-level sensing/driver loop.
A full-bank state transfer surface now spans−31/0/+31mV over13 geometries×8 biases. For higher gate fractions≥0.8, its transfer change has p10/median/p90 about−0.36/+0.017/+0.388%; inferred correction slope varies strongly with geometry and bias (p10/median/p90−0.00218/0.00263/0.01541/V). Ideal-state open-loop correction is not reliable. A behavioral bias source that reads the actual simulated membrane differential gives a better high-cancellation trace at0.003/V: final errors+14.37/−2.91/+4.84µV and no sampled sign errors, with up to0.169mV prefix error. Higher gain hits transient convergence and the same feedback barely helps the329-event near-threshold site. This establishes state-sensing as the useful control variable, not a selected circuit. A real analog sensor, bias driver and noise/bandwidth model must replace the behavioral source before macro selection.


#### Threshold readout corner follow-up — 2026-10-02

The regenerative latch can use a small clocked PMOS input pair to retain low-common-mode sensitivity, but sizing alone is not robust over the SKY130 SS/TT/FF bracket: the minimum legal assist passes the selected float-derived decision at TT/FF and fails at SS; the unassisted latch also fails SS. Opposite ideal threshold corrections are needed at SS and FF. Treat this as evidence for a physical auto-zero or differential trim reference, not as proof of one. See `physical_active_bucket_complementary_input_latch_screen` in the architecture boundary JSON.


The full-float final-fusion trace contains a dense near-threshold tail under the current analog voltage mapping: q0.1 absolute margin is0.537µV overall and0.264µV for spiking candidates. This makes offset/noise accuracy a system gate, not just a latch corner detail. The mapping is exploratory and must be recalibrated with a selected analog scale. See `original_float_threshold_margin_distribution` in the boundary JSON.


The current full-float replay favors pursuing an eight-segment nonlinear analog state mapping for the next experiment. At a 1000× range parameter, the ideal eight-segment map preserved over99.2% of pixels on two complete validation frames under0.1µV RMS synthetic threshold noise across all actors, while the per-actor linear map preserved89.4–90.6% on record0. Treat this only as encoding/noise sensitivity; the state-dependent analog update and range transitions are not implemented, and no PDK noise is measured.

2026-10-02 readout trim probe: retain a complementary two-ladder trim as the schematic candidate. In a physical MIM state/latch bench at 1000× exploratory state scaling, separate codes classified the ±0.537µV mapped cases for one deterministic paired ±1σ input-NFET mismatch and its mirror. A one-ladder VCM reference did not separate those cases. This does not select the full readout architecture: resistor matching/extraction, trim calibration, noise, other PVT corners, mapped full-model error replay, state retention and 5ms service remain unqualified. The first xhigh-poly PCell is DRC/LVS clean and measures1.225kΩ versus1.259kΩ nominal; no multi-resistor ladder layout has been extracted.

2026-10-02 resistor layout probe: a flattened, extracted two-resistor series layout passes Magic DRC and unique Netgen LVS, but its2.165kΩ DC value is14% below the sum of two PCell nominal values. This conflicts with the isolated resistor PCell result and leaves the physical trim DAC unqualified. Resolve the netlist/model scaling discrepancy before using PCell nominal values to design a larger resistor ladder.

Model follow-up localizes the apparent resistor shortfall to Magic's flattening model selection: generic `res_xhigh_po` predicts1.083kΩ per resistor, while the size-specific `res_xhigh_po_1p41` wrapper predicts1.225kΩ. The exact intended extraction flow remains to be established. Hierarchical extraction preserves the wrapper but the parent IN/OUT pins are not connected in the exported netlist; fix this before promoting the series segment.

2026-10-02 update: model mapping resolved the two-series-resistor discrepancy. The flat extracted connectivity is retained, and the devices map to the exact `res_xhigh_po_1p41` model named in the source PCell. The extracted mapped result is2.450602kΩ,2.68% below nominal; DRC0 and unique LVS pass. Keep the raw generic extraction as a comparison artifact. Next extend the cell to a multi-tap ladder and characterize switch-loaded transfer and ratio mismatch.

2026-10-02 resistor ladder primitive: extended the PCell-specific mapping to a flat three-segment string with physical IN/TAP1/TAP2/OUT pins. Magic DRC0, unique LVS, GDS generated; each segment measures1.225298kΩ and the sum3.675894kΩ at TT/27°C. Identical PCells show zero spread in this deterministic deck, which is not mismatch evidence. Continue with loaded tap transfer and a switched R-2R branch before the 24-bit DAC.

2026-10-02: The mapped three-tap resistor cell gives only−0.170/−0.158mV tap shift under10MΩ loads. A2-bit code-point simulation with layout-derived resistors and schematic TGs is monotonic, but step sizes differ by roughly1.7:1. Treat the ladder as a functional branch lead only; switch layout/extraction and resistor ratio/mismatch may further change linearity.


2026-10-02 primitive update: a DRC/LVS-qualified1µm NFET +2µm PFET SKY130 TG now has reusable extracted GDS/SPICE views, exposed bulk pins,10 coupling caps and a2.11×7.59µm footprint. Its2-bit ladder integration is monotonic at TT/SS/FF, but code steps are nonuniform; Ron peaks at9.4kΩ TT and34.1kΩ SS near0.9V. An extracted MIM-bank hold still drifts0.10–0.18mV over5ms due to the off switch. Keep this cell for architecture experiments, but it does not meet active full-float precision/retention and must not be scaled as the design's state or weight DAC. Full-float active work remains a shared differential charge bucket plus an unresolved scalable state store/readout; no QAT/digital fallback or full-chip layout is introduced.


CMFB actuator sizing screen at FF/85°C with the extracted TG: the stock0.42/10µm PMOS assist leaves the final output common mode near0.423V; increasing to2/1µm moves it only to0.507V, and2/0.15µm reaches0.802V. The last case still ends with about−0.205mV differential error on the shuffled high-cancellation sequence. This actuator-only tweak improves common mode but corrupts the useful differential state by orders of magnitude beyond full-float margin. Reject sizing alone as the fix; the active path needs a better matched differential integrator and common-mode servo topology before layout.
# Shared differential OTA-C state bucket: controller polarity screen (2026-10-02)

The output PMOS load gate has ample common-mode authority, but the first shared OTA servo is not qualified. DC sweeps across TT/SS/FF show that `mean > VCM` must raise the shared PMOS gate; this is obtained by connecting `VCM` to the CMFB OTA `inp` and the sensed mean to `inn`. Reversing those inputs causes positive feedback and rails outputs near VDD. The correct-sign transient still drifts/oscillates during 5ms and its DC transition is nearly rail-to-rail around 0.9V. Retain this as a polarity result only. Reduce controller gain/bandwidth, re-establish common mode, then test event charge and full-float margins before layout. Evidence: `scripts/screen_sky130_cmfb_ota_polarity.py` and its JSON report; no state accuracy, PVT mismatch/noise, PEX or full design pass follows from this experiment.

A resistor-only common-mode feedback variant was also screened. It stabilizes the mean but couples differential state through both feedback resistors: a1mV initialized differential state falls below0.105mV by100µs and reaches zero by1ms. Common mode is strongly corner-dependent; at a0.812V bias reference it gives0.907V TT,0.461V SS and1.728V FF. This is not a membrane implementation. Future differential state architecture needs common-mode correction that does not load or collapse the differential signal.

Source degeneration and compensation reduced the shared OTA servo's oscillation in one TT setting (100Ω per input leg,100pF compensation,0.45V tail bias), but SS collapsed the output to0.0003V and FF ended at0.662V. Thus this small-signal tuning is not a PVT-qualified control loop. A robust controller needs a defined linear operating region and enough process-independent range; no layout evidence is warranted yet.

The active state architecture is now exploring direct differential switched-capacitor charge storage: separate MIM state capacitors receive matched opposite packet steps through SKY130 TGs, keeping mean voltage near VCM without a continuous CMFB amplifier. The schematic PVT screen passes ± sign and4.99ms retention at TT/SS/FF; equal-and-opposite events cancel within0.1µV. The analog top-plate voltage remains ideal, the sampled PVT charge gain varies about−8% to+15%, and there is no mismatch/noise/readout/full-float replay or extracted cell. Proceed to paired layout and extracted simulation, followed by actual float trace mapping before selecting this as the macro.

2026-10-02 active storage note: the DRC/LVS-clean SKY130 differential MOS gate-cap pair is a storage primitive, not a complete PLIF. Its common-mode-correct AC result is14.45pF per rail at zero state and14.39–25.71pF over the measured±0.45V rail offsets. The separately measured100×10µm bank holds a±100mV differential state to within126–138µV over5ms at SS/TT/FF with its injector network isolated. Ideal piecewise8 full-float replay after this retention gives96.68–96.79% pixel agreement and about0.49% final-fuse spike mismatch on two frames. The20×2µm lower-area version is about0.30m² bare pair area but only91.9% best replay agreement. The100×10µm pair approaches the MIM bank's≈0.76m² bare estimate. Physical update, state sensing, comparator/readout, event overlap, mismatch/noise and full-inventory service remain unresolved, so no full neuron or chip architecture is selected.


### Shared-reset timing and reset-noise replay — 2026-10-02

Opening the inter-rail bridge while both VCM clamps remain on reduces the deterministic differential kick in the extracted TG/pair transient from 1.243µV to 0.132nV. The rails stay clamped for another 10µs before release. This is a sequencing result only: the deterministic simulator has no thermal noise or mismatch, and the independent-reset comparator also remains near zero in that model. It does not establish correlated reset noise. Reports and deck are `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_ss0` and `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_clamp_last_ss0`.

Replayed one full DSEC frame from the verified epoch58 full-float checkpoint with measured TT 5ms pair retention, ideal piecewise8 state encoding, and illustrative independent 24µV RMS differential reset noise. Three seeds with noise only at frame initialization gave 99.931–99.939% pixel agreement at range gain100, 99.918–99.919% at1000, and99.864% at10000. Reapplying the same noise after every subtractive spike reset gave99.927–99.935%,99.918–99.919%, and99.863–99.865%, respectively. Final-fusion spike mismatch remained below0.10%; labeled pixel accuracy remained near the float baseline49.82%. These results show the previous conclusion that 24µV reset noise necessarily destroys frame fidelity was tied to a less suitable state map/gain case. This replay remains a sensitivity test: piecewise encoding/update is ideal, the Gaussian is not a PDK transient-noise waveform, and the single frame does not qualify the circuit.

Next characterize the physical noise and state-dependent error of the extracted event injector against the original-float high-cancellation, maximum-fanin and near-threshold sequences. Build/extract a reset-and-state macro only after the injector and readout topology are credible; then run PVT/noise and replay measured errors. No full PLIF or chip acceptance is claimed.


### Extracted float-weight MOS injector sequence gate — 2026-10-02

Replayed the 322-event epoch58 high-cancellation final-fusion fan-in through the extracted SKY130 ten-device MOS state banks and 13-geometry signed injector at SS/0°C. With the existing ideal per-event bias commands, 4.4µs transfer pulses and 5µs slots, the shuffled full sequence ends at −49.77µV versus the full-float target +5.442µV: −55.22µV error and a final sign mismatch. The equivalent 40-event prefix ends −6.430µV from target; 2.5ns and10ns maximum timesteps agree within0.003µV, establishing numerical convergence for that prefix. A simple behavioral bidirectional state-feedback slope of0.004/V worsens the full shuffled error to−63.81µV. Measured-state-transfer correction on the first40 events worsens error to−102.56µV, while the ideal polarity correction leaves it near−6.59µV. These results do not support taking the current long-pulse MOS injector into a PLIF layout. They are bounded one-site analog screens, without physical weight DAC, mismatch/noise, threshold/reset/β or full network. Evidence is in `sky130_ms_mos_state_injector_boundary_high_cancellation_shuffle_layoutbank_dt10.json`, `_n40_dt2p5.json`, `_n40_dt10.json`, the state-feedback report and `_n40` correction reports.

Continue with the active differential charge bucket / switched-capacitor event path as the leading analog alternative. First characterize full-float weighted event order, near-threshold output, physical weight delivery, PVT, readout and retention together; reuse existing DRC/LVS-clean primitive cells where applicable. Do not scale the current MOS injector or claim a selected neuron macro yet.

2026-10-02 CMFB boundary: the current duplicated single-ended OTA buckets are not a qualified PLIF. Extracted-TG near-threshold replay gives +37.78µV against +32.62µV float target with no servo, while CM drifts 16.1mV; a stronger shared PMOS assist restores CM but changes the differential update to −182.66µV. The next candidate is a monolithic fully differential amplifier with internal common-mode sensing/control, not an output-side assist. Reuse qualified MIM and TG primitives; retain analog full-float behavior and gate layout on measured sequence/PVT/readout fidelity.

2026-10-02: additional extracted-TG active-bucket diagnostics at two original-float operating sites across TT/SS/FF disqualify the current OTA bucket for layout. High cancellation is dominated by a 4–5µV circuit residual against a7.49nV float update. Max-fanin error ranges+0.181mV/−0.568mV/+2.139mV at TT/SS/FF and FF common mode collapses to0.351V. A single +2.028mV event decays to+1.450mV at FF by5ms. Preserve the result artifacts; next compare the DRC/LVS-clean retained MOS-cap bank and passive MIM packet primitive against the actual full-float event/update margins. No full-neuron or chip layout is approved by these results.

2026-10-02 event-gated OTA architecture screen: switching the active OTA output off between updates improves stored differential retention, and a4µs aperture brings TT max-fanin update within2.55% of ideal. Yet common mode drifts to0.706V TT and collapses to0.021V FF; high-cancellation residual is still600× the ideal final update. Keep this as a rejected experimental cell, not a macro candidate. The next step is to choose from the already characterized retained MOS-cap and passive MIM paths using actual float thresholds and area/service; a combined cell/full-chip assembly remains blocked on an accurate, stable update element.

2026-10-02 readout follow-up: the 100-device/side differential MOS sensor has20.31µV integrated input noise over10Hz–1MHz TT and causes only0.13nV excess drift in the previous nominal5ms MOS-bank loading experiment (that loading deck uses the smaller sensor). These are complementary but separate simulations. The larger sensor noise is below one prior illustrative24µV state-noise sensitivity, but has no sampled-noise/mismatch/trim/area/mux qualification. Preserve shared differential sensing as a readout candidate while the state-update path is redesigned.

The updated sampled-autozero/readout boundary analyzer now combines the 20.31µV/1MHz sensor-noise result with the 16,108 parallel-latch lower bound: the 100-device/side differential sensor adds about96.97mm² bare active-device area alone. This is much smaller than per-identity sampled offset storage, but nontrivial and excludes mux/routing/control. Treat it as an architectural bound, not a completed floorplan.

The42.3ns extracted-MOS-state replay demonstrates that open-loop current injection still fails at the actual high-cancellation workload: all three event orders converge to about−68nV against+7.49nV float, while a±50µV stored-state change modulates the low-geometry source by several percent and can reverse its weakest pulse. A microvolt-accurate local state sensor is unsupported by the current noise/area frontier. The next analog experiment is therefore a closed-loop fully differential OTA-C event integrator with virtual-ground input summing and internal CMFB; no existing active-bucket result qualifies this topology.

2026-10-03 next experiment: build a matched charge-balanced packet path with dummy TGs so both differential state rails see the same switching and top-plate transitions for every signed float event. Replay the same 322-event high-cancellation sequence in shuffle/forward/reverse at TT and SS, measure state at bin checkpoints, then screen max-fanin range. Existing dual same-sign reservoir fails and is out of layout selection. A candidate must pass update/error and 5ms hold before integrated layout; standalone qualified MIM/TG GDS remains available.

2026-10-03 amplitude-injector calibration update: fixed-width MOS gate-amplitude encoding now reproduces one 370-event max-fanin sum at four same-corner temperature points to0.11–2.19% using a matching isolated-event lookup; the original near-threshold site event sum is within1.83% TT/27C,1.62% SS/0C and1.45% FF/85C. Without matching the corner map, SS and FF errors were36% and65%. This is still an ideal-source schematic experiment. Continue with physical analog weight storage, transfer/trim generation and extracted injector routing; then measure mismatch/noise/state dependence and rerun original float sites. Keep existing qualified MIM/TG primitives, but do not lay out an integrated neuron until the state storage/readout/service gates pass.

2026-10-03 static geometry weight follow-up: the segmented runner had been restarting each chunk at the DC operating point; its apparent 23% full-sequence error is invalid as state-carry evidence. Correct UIC starts exposed rapid off-state loading in the direct geometry bank: with a 100µm state cap the differential moved 2.90mV during the 90ns before the first event. A per-branch series NMOS access switch reduced that pre-event change to 34nV, and a polarity-reversed, 5× widened single-event case delivered +0.695mV against +0.683mV target (1.81% error) at SS/0°C. This is one isolated event only: the 20-event fan-in deck fails because the largest geometry-scaled MOS exceeds the PDK model range, and an attempted 5-finger instance aborts at transient startup. Do not treat the geometry bank as an accepted accumulator. Corrected scripts now require the ngspice completion marker and reject transient aborts, and reports record requested initial state voltages. Next measure an in-range isolated-access transfer grid and off-state retention, then replay ordered float fan-in only if both pass; integrated layout remains gated.

Follow-up qualification boundary (2026-10-03): with in-range geometry widths and a PEX-style series transmission gate, the one-event sweep at headroom0.2–0.9 delivers only34–20% of the float event charge (66–80% low). Widening the transmission gate from10 to40µm improves one-event charge from0.115mV to0.357mV against0.683mV, while common mode shifts up to2.7mV. A prior 5× raw MOS width case matched one event but exceeded the PDK geometry range in the full-fanin deck; reject that apparent scaling solution. Instantiating all360 branches was killed by the bounded Docker memory limit, and a33-branch attempt hit timestep underflow at the event edge. These are simulator/bench limits plus an actual underdriven event; no full sequence or integrated layout passes. Next proceed with a convergent isolated-switch driver or the shared calibrated injector physical weight-source build, preserving the analog full-float reference.

Series-isolated pulse-width control check (2026-10-03): the first float event was sampled1ns after the pulse at10.5/30/52.5ns. Measured differential increments were0.230/0.217/0.202mV against+0.683mV target (−66.4/−68.3/−70.4%); common mode shifted+0.69mV. This confirms the in-range underdrive is not caused by the former100ns observation delay, and extending pulse width makes transfer slightly worse. Preserve full-float event source and investigate a differently biased current injector or shared physical DAC path; do not layout the isolated geometry cell.

Shared bias drive is a peripheral analog circuit used to set injector operating points. Its 12-bit DAC code is not a quantized network weight and no QAT model is used. Present TT-trimmed transfer differs by up to about6% at SS; the ideal-resistor bench and 2-bit extracted resistor/TG check are insufficient for deployment. A physical resistor ladder, calibration across PVT, stored analog weight delivery and SNN sequence validation remain open.

The physical resistor slice establishes the ladder geometry and extraction flow, not a finished high-resolution DAC. A 2-bit passive slice with ideal bit rails is highly linear; the extracted base TG devices distort code steps up to37% across process corners. Device multiplicity improves the simulated transfer but has not been physically laid out. A high-drive switch bank plus physical parasitics and mismatch must pass before this path can drive calibrated analog full-float event weights.
